<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the finite-rational-coefficient case of the effective log Iitaka conjecture in dimension four. For normal projective log canonical complex fourfolds with boundary coefficients in a fixed finite rational set and pseudo-effective rational Cartier adjoint, one uniform degree makes the complete rounded reflexive system nonempty and its section ratios generate the full Iitaka field. We also prove a uniform canonical index bound for projective klt complex fourfolds with rationally trivial canonical divisor.

- **claim-uniform-canonical-index** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-fourfold-index-zero)): There is a positive integer $N_4$ such that $N_4K_X\sim0$ for every normal projective klt complex fourfold $X$ with $K_X\sim_\mathbb Q0$.
  - Explanation: One integer trivializes the canonical class of every normal projective klt complex fourfold with rationally trivial canonical divisor.
- **claim-uniform-log-iitaka** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $\Phi\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$ be finite. There is a positive integer $m=m(\Phi)$ such that the following holds. Suppose $X$ is a normal integral projective complex fourfold, $\Delta\geq0$ is a rational Weil divisor whose nonzero coefficients lie in $\Phi$, $(X,\Delta)$ is lc, and $D=K_X+\Delta$ is rational Cartier and pseudo-effective. Then the complete system $\|\lfloor mD\rfloor\|$ is nonempty and its section ratios generate $K(D)$ inside $\mathbb C(X)$. The same integer works for every choice of canonical divisor on $X$.
  - Explanation: For a finite rational coefficient set one degree makes the complete rounded system nonempty and generating the full Iitaka field.
- **claim-abundance-after-nonvanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-fourfold)): Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a projective lc rational pair over $\mathbb C$ of dimension at most four, with effective boundary and nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier adjoint $D=K_X+\Delta$. If $\kappa(X,D)\ge0$, then $D$ is semiample. If $\kappa(X,D)=0$, then $D\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$.
  - Explanation: A nef adjoint of nonnegative Kodaira dimension on an lc rational pair of dimension at most four is semiample.
- **claim-semiample-model-comparison** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-semiample-model)): Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a projective lc rational pair of dimension four over $\mathbb C$, with $D=K_X+\Delta$ $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and $\kappa(X,D)\geq0$. There is a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt pair $(X',\Delta')$ with semiample adjoint $D'=K_{X'}+\Delta'$ such that, under the identification of rational function fields and compatible canonical divisors, $$\label{eq:all-rounded-sections} H^0\bigl(X,\mathcal O_X(\lfloor mD\rfloor)\bigr) =H^0\bigl(X',\mathcal O_{X'}(\lfloor mD'\rfloor)\bigr) \qquad(m\geq1).$$ The coefficients of $\Delta'$ belong to those of $\Delta$ together with $1$. The map is obtained by a crepant dlt modification followed by adjoint-negative birational steps. For a $\mathbb Q$-factorial klt input the latter steps can be taken directly, with klt outputs. The semiample contraction $f:X'\to Z$ has $\dim Z=\kappa(X,D)$ and $$D'\sim_{\mathbb Q}f^*A$$ for an ample rational Cartier divisor $A$ on the normal projective variety $Z$.
  - Explanation: A crepant dlt modification and adjoint-negative steps give a dlt model with semiample adjoint and identical rounded section spaces.
- **claim-multiples-generate-full-field** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-multiples-full-field)): Let $D$ be a rational Cartier divisor on a normal projective variety such that $D\sim_{\mathbb Q}f^*A$ for a contraction $f:X\to Z$ and an ample rational Cartier divisor $A$. If one nonempty complete system $\|\lfloor mD\rfloor\|$ generates $\mathbb C(Z)$ inside $\mathbb C(X)$, then every positive integer multiple of $m$ does so as well.
  - Explanation: Field generation by one complete rounded system persists for every positive multiple of its degree.
- **claim-uniform-relative-denominators** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-relative-denominators)): Fix a finite set $\Phi\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$ and an integer $1\leq d\leq4$. There are positive integers $p_0\mid p$, with $p_0$ clearing $\Phi$, and a DCC set $\mathcal B\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$, depending only on $d$ and $\Phi$, with the following property.* *Let $(X,B)$ be a projective lc pair of dimension $d$, with effective rational boundary having coefficients in $\Phi$. Suppose that $f:X\to Z$ is a contraction onto a projective base $Z$, with $\dim Z>0$, and $$K_X+B\sim_\mathbb Qf^*D$$ for a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $D$ on $Z$. There exist $\psi\in\mathbb C(X)^*$ and a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $D_Z\sim_\mathbb QD$ such that $$\label{eq:relative-exact-presentation} K_X+B+\frac1{p_0}\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(\psi)=f^*D_Z, \qquad D_Z=K_Z+B_Z+M_Z.$$ Here $B_Z$ is effective with coefficients in $\mathcal B$, $(Z,B_Z+M_Z)$ is generalized lc, and $\mathbf M$ is b-nef with $p\mathbf M$ b-Cartier. If $(X,B)$ is klt, the generalized pair on $Z$ is generalized klt.
  - Explanation: Uniform denominators give an exact presentation of the adjoint of a log Calabi–Yau fibration by a generalized lc pair on the base.
- **claim-effective-base-field** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-effective-base)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:relative-denominators" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:relative-denominators">6</a>, suppose in addition that $D$ is big. There is a positive integer $m=m(d,\Phi)$ such that, for every positive multiple $l$ of $m$, the complete divisorial system $$\left\|\left\lfloor l(K_X+B)\right\rfloor\right\|$$ is nonempty and generates precisely the subfield $\mathbb C(Z)\subset\mathbb C(X)$. Its rational map is therefore birationally equivalent to $f$ and to the Iitaka fibration of $K_X+B$.
  - Explanation: Over a big base, every multiple of one uniform degree gives a nonempty complete system generating exactly the base field.
- **claim-rc-torsion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-rc-torsion)): Fix an integer $1\leq d\leq 4$, a DCC set $I\subset [0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$, and a positive integer $p$. There exists a positive integer $\ell=\ell(d,I,p)$ with the following property. Let $Z$ be a normal projective complex variety of dimension $d$ with a rationally connected smooth projective resolution. Suppose that $(Z,B+M_Z)$ is generalized klt, that $B\geq 0$ has coefficients in $I$, and that the b-nef rational b-divisor $\mathbf M$ has $p\mathbf M$ b-Cartier. If $$D=K_Z+B+M_Z\sim_{\mathbb Q}0,$$ then $\ell D$ is an integral principal divisor. Consequently, for every integer $a\geq1$, the divisor $a\ell D$ is principal and $$h^0\bigl(Z,\mathcal O_Z(a\ell D)\bigr)=1.$$ The same integer can be chosen for all dimensions in any fixed subset of $\{1,2,3,4\}$.
  - Explanation: On a base with rationally connected resolution a rationally trivial generalized klt adjoint is trivialized by a uniform multiple.
- **claim-index-product-case** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-index-product-case)): There is a uniform positive integer $N_{\rm prod}$ with the following property. If a projective klt fourfold $X$, with $K_X\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$, admits a cover as in <a href="#eq:index-product-cover" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:index-product-cover">[eq:index-product-cover]</a> having at least two positive dimensional factors, or having only an abelian factor, then $N_{\rm prod}K_X\sim0$.
  - Explanation: A uniform index bound holds when the product cover has two positive dimensional factors or only an abelian factor.
- **claim-single-factor-images** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-index-single-factor-images)): Suppose that a projective klt fourfold $X$, with $K_X\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$, has a finite quasi-étale cover $P\to X$ in which $P$ is one four-dimensional irreducible Calabi–Yau or irreducible holomorphic symplectic factor as above. Then the following statements hold.* 1. *There is no dominant rational map from $X$ to a positive dimensional non-uniruled smooth projective variety of dimension less than four.* 2. *Every positive dimensional rational image of dimension less than four has rationally connected smooth projective resolution.* 3. *Every smooth projective model of $X$ has irregularity zero.
  - Explanation: With one four-dimensional nonflat factor, no non-uniruled lower-dimensional images exist, images are rationally connected and irregularity vanishes.
- **claim-exceptional-psef-effective** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-index-exceptional-psef)): Let $u:W\to X$ be a projective birational morphism from a smooth projective fourfold to a normal projective variety. A pseudo-effective $u$-exceptional real divisor on $W$ is effective.
  - Explanation: A pseudo-effective exceptional real divisor is effective.
- **claim-noncanonical-orders** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-index-noncanonical)): The canonical orders of the noncanonical varieties in Lemma <a href="#lem:index-single-factor-images" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:index-single-factor-images">10</a> are uniformly bounded.
  - Explanation: Noncanonical varieties of the single-factor lemma have uniformly bounded canonical orders.
- **claim-crepant-terminalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-index-terminalization)): A normal projective canonical variety $X$ with $K_X\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$ has a projective crepant $\mathbb Q$-factorial terminalization $V\to X$. The canonical orders of $V$ and $X$ are equal.
  - Explanation: A canonical variety with rationally trivial canonical class has a crepant terminalization with the same canonical order.
- **claim-moving-divisor-index** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-index-moving-divisor)): There is a uniform positive integer $N_{\rm mov}$ with the following property. Let $V$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal fourfold with $K_V\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$, and suppose that every positive dimensional rational image of dimension less than four has rationally connected smooth projective resolution. If $V$ has an effective Weil divisor $D$ with $1\le\kappa(V,D)\le3$, then $N_{\rm mov}K_V\sim0$.
  - Explanation: An effective divisor of intermediate Iitaka dimension forces a uniformly bounded canonical order on a terminal fourfold.
- **claim-general-type-fibres** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-index-general-type-fibres)): Let $V$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal fourfold with $K_V\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$. Suppose that every effective Weil divisor on $V$ of positive Iitaka dimension is big. Let $\pi:Y\to V$ be its cyclic index cover, with deck group $G$. Then every $G$-equivariant rational fibration of $Y$ with positive dimensional base and fibre has a geometric generic fibre of general type on a smooth resolution. The same conclusion holds for every such rational fibration of $V$.
  - Explanation: If every effective divisor of positive Iitaka dimension is big, equivariant rational fibrations have generic fibres of general type.
- **claim-generic-chains** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-generic-chain)): Let a nonempty finite list of marked covering families be given on an integral projective variety $V$ in characteristic zero. There is a dominant rational map $$\xi:V\dashrightarrow M$$ with geometrically integral generic fibre, whose fibre closures $H_p\ni p$ have a constant positive dimension $e$ for general $p$. Starting at a generic $p$, a path of at most $e$ generic movements reaches a point generic in $H_p$ over the algebraic closure of the field of $p$. Every generic movement preserves the value of $\xi$. In particular, a generic family member $C_b$ is contained in $H_{u(b)}$.* *The assignment is equivariant under any finite automorphism group preserving the family list.
  - Explanation: Marked covering families define a rational quotient whose fibre closures are leaves reached by boundedly many generic movements.
- **claim-chain-functoriality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-chain-functoriality)): Suppose a dominant rational map between two ambient varieties sends generic movements of an upper family list to generic movements of a lower family list, or to stationary steps. Then the image of a generic upper leaf is contained in the corresponding lower leaf.* *If a finite group preserves a family list on $V$ and $\theta:V\to V'$ is its quotient, the subvarieties $\theta(H_p)$ are the closures of the geometrically integral generic fibres of a rational fibration on $V'$.
  - Explanation: Chain leaves are compatible with dominant maps respecting family lists and with finite quotients.
- **claim-chain-order-degree** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-chain-order)): Let the marked families and their rational quotient be as in Lemma <a href="#lem:generic-chain" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:generic-chain">16</a>, and let $e$ be the dimension of a generic leaf. Suppose that $P$ is a big, nef, semiample rational Cartier divisor on $V$. Assume that, for every family, its geometric generic marked member satisfies one of the following bounds, with a common number $B\ge0$:* 1. *the mark is smooth on $C_b$ and $\gamma(P;C_b,u(b))\le B$;* 2. *$C_b$ is a curve and $P\cdot C_b/\mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_{u(b)}C_b\le B$.* *Then on the geometric generic leaf, $$\label{eq:chain-order-degree} \gamma(P;H_p)\le eB, \qquad P^e\cdot H_p\le(eB)^e.$$
  - Explanation: Marked-member bounds propagate along chains to bound section order and degree of a generic leaf.
- **claim-scalar-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-scalar-bounds)): There are constants $C,c>0$ with the following property. Let $V$ be a normal projective canonical fourfold with $K_V\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$, and let a finite group $G$ act on $V$. Suppose that every $G$-equivariant rational fibration with geometrically integral generic fibre and with both base and fibre of positive dimension has geometric generic fibre of general type on a smooth projective resolution. It is enough to impose this condition on the rational quotients supplied by Lemma <a href="#lem:generic-chain" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:generic-chain">16</a>. If $P$ is an ample rational Cartier divisor satisfying $g^*P\sim_{\mathbb Q}P$ for every $g\in G$, then $$\label{eq:scalar-bounds} \gamma(P;V)\le C(P^4)^{1/4}, \qquad \varepsilon(P;x)\ge c(P^4)^{1/4}$$ at a very general smooth point $x\in V$, where $$\varepsilon(P;x)= \inf_{\substack{x\in D\subset V\\D\text{ an integral curve}}} \frac{P\cdot D}{\mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_xD}.$$ The constants are independent of $V$, $G$, and $P$.
  - Explanation: Section order is bounded above and curve ratio below by multiples of the fourth root of the volume, uniformly.
- **claim-scalar-subadjunction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-subadjunction)): Let $Z$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial klt variety, let $\Theta\ge0$ be a rational divisor, and let $S\subset Z$ be an integral subvariety of dimension $d>0$. Suppose that $(Z,\Theta)$ is lc over a neighbourhood of the generic point of $S$, and that $S$ is an lc centre there. For a nef rational Cartier divisor $A$ on $Z$ and a smooth projective resolution $S^*\to S$, one has $$\label{eq:scalar-subadjunction} K_{S^*}\cdot(A\|_{S^*})^{d-1} \le (K_Z+\Theta)\|_S\cdot(A\|_S)^{d-1}.$$ Here the right-hand side means the intersection of the restricted rational line bundle with the cycle $S$.
  - Explanation: Numerical subadjunction bounds canonical intersections on a resolution of an lc centre.
- **claim-scalar-tracking** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-tracking)): Let $Z$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial klt variety of dimension $n\ge2$, and let $N$ be a big nef semiample rational Cartier divisor. Suppose there are $n+1$ positive rational numbers, with consecutive spacing $\delta>0$, all strictly between $(N^n)^{1/n}$ and $\gamma(N;Z)$. There is a covering family of integral subvarieties $S\subset Z$ of some dimension $0<d<n$ such that, on a smooth projective resolution $S^*$ of its geometric generic member, $$\begin{aligned} N^d\cdot S&\le (2/\delta)^{n-d}N^n, \label{eq:scalar-track-degree}\\ K_{S^*}\cdot(N\|_{S^*})^{d-1} &\le \bigl(K_Z+(2n/\delta)N\bigr)\|_S \cdot(N\|_S)^{d-1}. \label{eq:scalar-track-canonical} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Spaced jet levels yield a covering family of lower-dimensional subvarieties with explicit degree and canonical-degree bounds.
- **claim-covering-member-dimension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-covering)): Let $X$ be a projective variety birational to a smooth projective variety with a nonzero pluricanonical section. A smooth projective resolution of the geometric generic member of a covering family of integral subvarieties of $X$ has nonnegative Kodaira dimension. If $X$ is of general type, that resolution is of general type.
  - Explanation: Resolutions of generic covering members have nonnegative Kodaira dimension, and general type when the ambient variety is.
- **claim-iitaka-fibre-volume** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-iitaka-volume)): Fix $1\le d\le3$ and a positive rational number $C'$. Let $(W_i,L_i)$ be a sequence of smooth projective $d$-folds with $\kappa(W_i)\ge0$ and big, nef, semiample rational Cartier divisors $L_i$. Suppose that $$\label{eq:scalar-fibre-hypotheses} L_i^d\longrightarrow0, \qquad K_{W_i}\cdot L_i^{d-1}\le C'L_i^d.$$ After passing to a subsequence and resolving the Iitaka fibrations, their smooth geometric generic fibres $U_i$ have a fixed positive dimension, satisfy $\kappa(U_i)=0$, and obey $$\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(K_{U_i}+L_i\|_{U_i})\longrightarrow0.$$ Here $L_i$ denotes its pullback to the resolved model; its restriction to $U_i$ is big, nef, and semiample. When $\kappa(W_i)=0$, the fibre $U_i$ means $W_i$ itself.
  - Explanation: For sequences with vanishing polarized volume and bounded canonical degree, Iitaka fibres have Kodaira dimension zero and vanishing adjoint volume.
- **claim-quadratic-jet-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-quadratic-cost)): Let $X$ be a normal projective canonical fourfold over $\mathbb C$ with $K_X\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$, and let $r$ be the least positive integer such that $rK_X\sim0$. Let $\pi:Y\to X$ be its canonical cyclic cover, with group $\mu_r$. Fix $C_1\in\mathbb Q_{>0}$, and choose $b_0\in\mathbb Q_{>0}$ satisfying $$\label{eq:frob-choice-b0} 3(2b_0)^4<\frac14, \qquad b_0(C_1+1)<\frac34.$$ Suppose that $L$ is an ample rational Cartier divisor on $X$ such that $$1\le L^4\le2, \qquad \gamma(L;X)\le C_1,$$ where $\gamma$ is as in Definition <a href="#def:orders" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:orders">18</a>. For each integer $t\ge2$, set $$Z_t=Y^t/\mu_{r,\mathrm{diag}}, \qquad \theta_t:Z_t\longrightarrow X^t, \qquad P_t=\theta_t^*\left(\sum_{i=1}^t\operatorname{pr}_i^*L\right).$$ Then $P_t$ is ample and, at a very general point $z$ of $Z_t$, $$\label{eq:frob-cost} \varepsilon(P_t;z)\le\frac{5t^2}{b_0}.$$
  - Explanation: On the diagonal quotient of products of the canonical cyclic cover the curve ratio is bounded quadratically in the number of factors, independently of the cover order.
- **claim-frobenius-truncated-slopes** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-frob-truncated-slopes)): Let $C$ be a smooth projective integral curve of genus $g$ over an algebraically closed field $k$ of characteristic $p>0$. Let $V$ be a vector bundle of rank $n$ with $\mu_{\min}(V)\ge0$, and put $$G=\max\{0,2g-2\},\qquad u=n(p-1),\qquad c_2=n(n-1)G.$$ Let $A_\bullet(V)$ denote the symmetric algebra of $V$ modulo the ideal locally generated by the $p$-th powers of its linear generators. Thus, in a local frame, $A_\bullet(V)$ is the graded algebra $\mathcal O_C[v_1,\ldots,v_n]/(v_1^p,\ldots,v_n^p)$. Its graded pieces are locally free, it is zero in degrees above $u$, and $$\label{eq:frob-truncated-upper-slope} \mu_{\max}\bigl(A_j(V)\bigr) \le (p-1)\deg(V)+c_2 \qquad (0\le j\le u).$$
  - Explanation: Graded pieces of the truncated symmetric algebra of a semipositive bundle have explicitly bounded maximal slope.
- **claim-frobenius-flag-sections** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-frob-flag-sections)): Let $W$ be a smooth projective fourfold over an algebraically closed field, and let $H$ be an ample rational Cartier divisor. Fix a smooth complete intersection flag, cut successively by divisors from $\|l_1H\|$, $\|l_2H\|$, and $\|l_3H\|$, ending in a smooth integral curve $C$; here each $l_iH$ is an integral ample divisor. Write $d_i=l_iH\cdot C$. If $E$ is a vector bundle of rank $e$ and $\mu_{\max}(E\|_C)\le M$ for a real number $M\ge0$, then $$\label{eq:frob-flag-sections} h^0(W,E)\le e(M+1)\prod_{i=1}^3\left(1+\frac{M}{d_i}\right).$$
  - Explanation: Global sections of a bundle on a fourfold are bounded through its maximal slope on a flag curve.
- **claim-frobenius-external-orders** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-frob-external-orders)): For $1\le i\le t$, let $V_i$ be an integral projective variety over an algebraically closed field, let $x_i\in V_i$ be a smooth point, and let $M_i$ be a line bundle with $H^0(V_i,M_i)\ne0$. Put $$a_i=\max_{0\ne s\in H^0(V_i,M_i)}\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{x_i}(s).$$ Every nonzero section $s$ of the external product $\bigotimes_i\operatorname{pr}_i^*M_i$ satisfies $$\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{(x_1,\ldots,x_t)}(s)\le\sum_{i=1}^t a_i.$$
  - Explanation: The order of a section of an external product at a product point is at most the sum of the slot orders.
- **claim-many-factor-contradiction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-many-factor-contradiction)): There is no sequence of finite cyclic covers $$\pi_\nu:Y_\nu\longrightarrow X_\nu$$ of normal projective fourfolds, generically torsors for cyclic groups $G_\nu$ of orders $r_\nu\to\infty$, with the following properties. The group $\mathop{\mathrm{Bir}}(X_\nu)$ is countable, and there are ample rational Cartier divisors $L_\nu$ on $X_\nu$ and constants $c,C>0$, independent of $\nu$, such that at very general points $$\varepsilon(L_\nu)\ge c, \qquad \varepsilon(\pi_\nu^*L_\nu)\ge c r_\nu^{1/4}.$$ Moreover, for each fixed integer $t\ge2$, put $$Z_{\nu,t}=Y_\nu^t/G_{\nu,\mathrm{diag}},\qquad \theta_{\nu,t}:Z_{\nu,t}\longrightarrow X_\nu^t, \qquad P_{\nu,t}=\theta_{\nu,t}^*L_\nu^{\boxplus t}.$$ Here $L^{\boxplus t}=\sum_{i=1}^t\operatorname{pr}_i^*L$. The further required bound is $$\varepsilon(P_{\nu,t})\le Ct^2$$ at a very general point, for all sufficiently large $\nu$, with this threshold allowed to depend on $t$.
  - Explanation: A sequence of cyclic covers with growing orders, countable birational groups and the stated curve-ratio bounds cannot exist.
- **claim-birational-countability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-birational-countability)): Let $X$ be a normal projective terminal variety with $K_X\sim_\mathbb Q0$. If a smooth projective resolution of $X$ has irregularity zero, then $\mathop{\mathrm{Bir}}(X)$ is countable.
  - Explanation: A terminal variety with rationally trivial canonical class and irregularity-zero resolution has countable birational group.

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-rc-torsion",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $1\\leq d\\leq 4$, a DCC set $I\\subset [0,1]\\cap\\mathbb Q$, and a positive integer $p$. There exists a positive integer $\\ell=\\ell(d,I,p)$ with the following property. Let $Z$ be a normal projective complex variety of dimension $d$ with a rationally connected smooth projective resolution. Suppose that $(Z,B+M_Z)$ is generalized klt, that $B\\geq 0$ has coefficients in $I$, and that the b-nef rational b-divisor $\\mathbf M$ has $p\\mathbf M$ b-Cartier. If $$D=K_Z+B+M_Z\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0,$$ then $\\ell D$ is an integral principal divisor. Consequently, for every integer $a\\geq1$, the divisor $a\\ell D$ is principal and $$h^0\\bigl(Z,\\mathcal O_Z(a\\ell D)\\bigr)=1.$$ The same integer can be chosen for all dimensions in any fixed subset of $\\{1,2,3,4\\}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.13"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-generic-chain",
        "exact_quote": "In particular, a generic family member $C_b$ is contained in $H_{u(b)}$.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-generic-chain",
          "exact_quote": "Let a nonempty finite list of marked covering families be given on an integral projective variety $V$ in characteristic zero. There is a dominant rational map $$\\xi:V\\dashrightarrow M$$ with geometrically integral generic fibre, whose fibre closures $H_p\\ni p$ have a constant positive dimension $e$ for general $p$. Starting at a generic $p$, a path of at most $e$ generic movements reaches a point generic in $H_p$ over the algebraic closure of the field of $p$. Every generic movement preserves the value of $\\xi$. In particular, a generic family member $C_b$ is contained in $H_{u(b)}$.*\n\n*The assignment is equivariant under any finite automorphism group preserving the family list.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.13"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let a nonempty finite list of marked covering families be given on an integral projective variety $V$ in characteristic zero. There is a dominant rational map $$\\xi:V\\dashrightarrow M$$ with geometrically integral generic fibre, whose fibre closures $H_p\\ni p$ have a constant positive dimension $e$ for general $p$. Starting at a generic $p$, a path of at most $e$ generic movements reaches a point generic in $H_p$ over the algebraic closure of the field of $p$. Every generic movement preserves the value of $\\xi$. In particular, a generic family member $C_b$ is contained in $H_{u(b)}$.*\n\n*The assignment is equivariant under any finite automorphism group preserving the family list.",
      "explanation": "Marked covering families define a rational quotient whose fibre closures are leaves reached by boundedly many generic movements.",

        "exact_quote": "Let the marked families and their rational quotient be as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:generic-chain\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:generic-chain\">16</a>, and let $e$ be the dimension of a generic leaf. Assume that, for every family, its geometric generic marked member satisfies one of the following bounds, with a common number $B\\ge0$:*\n\n1.  *the mark is smooth on $C_b$ and $\\gamma(P;C_b,u(b))\\le B$;*\n\n2.  *$C_b$ is a curve and $P\\cdot C_b/\\mathop{\\mathrm{mult}}_{u(b)}C_b\\le B$.*\n\n\n*Then on the geometric generic leaf, $$\\label{eq:chain-order-degree}\n \\gamma(P;H_p)\\le eB,\n \\qquad\n P^e\\cdot H_p\\le(eB)^e.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.13"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "exact_quote": "Let the marked families and their rational quotient be as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:generic-chain\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:generic-chain\">16</a>, and let $e$ be the dimension of a generic leaf.           "source_id": "PM-034.13"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let the marked families and their rational quotient be as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:generic-chain\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:generic-chain\">16</a>, and let $e$ be the dimension of a generic leaf. Suppose that $P$ is a big, nef, semiample rational Cartier divisor on $V$. Assume that, for every family, its geometric generic marked member satisfies one of the following bounds, with a common number $B\\ge0$:*\n\n1.  *the mark is smooth on $C_b$ and $\\gamma(P;C_b,u(b))\\le B$;*\n\n2.  *$C_b$ is a curve and $P\\cdot C_b/\\mathop{\\mathrm{mult}}_{u(b)}C_b\\le B$.*\n\n\n*Then on the geometric generic leaf, $$\\label{eq:chain-order-degree}\n \\gamma(P;H_p)\\le eB,\n \\qquad\n P^e\\cdot H_p\\le(eB)^e.$$",
      "explanation": "Marked-member bounds propagate along chains to bound section order and degree of a generic leaf.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-scalar-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $C,c>0$ with the following property. Let $V$ be a normal projective canonical fourfold with $K_V\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$, and let a finite group $G$ act on $V$. Suppose that every $G$-equivariant rational fibration with geometrically integral generic fibre and with both base and fibre of positive dimension has geometric generic fibre of general type on a smooth projective resolution. It is enough to impose this condition on the rational quotients supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:generic-chain\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:generic-chain\">16</a>. If $P$ is an ample rational Cartier divisor satisfying $g^*P\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}P$ for every $g\\in G$, then $$\\label{eq:scalar-bounds}\n \\gamma(P;V)\\le C(P^4)^{1/4},\n \\qquad\n \\varepsilon(P;x)\\ge c(P^4)^{1/4}$$ at a very general smooth point $x\\in V$, where $$\\varepsilon(P;x)=\n \\inf_{\\substack{x\\in D\\subset V\\\\D\\text{ an integral curve}}}\n \\frac{P\\cdot D}{\\mathop{\\mathrm{mult}}_xD}.$$ The constants are independent of $V$, $G$, and $P$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.13"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-scalar-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $C,c>0$ with the following property. It is enough to impose this condition on the rational quotients supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:generic-chain\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:generic-chain\">16</a>. If $P$ is an ample rational Cartier divisor satisfying $g^*P\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}P$ for every $g\\in G$, then $$\\label{eq:scalar-bounds}\n \\gamma(P;V)\\le C(P^4)^{1/4},\n \\qquad\n \\varepsilon(P;x)\\ge c(P^4)^{1/4}$$ at a very general smooth point $x\\in V$, where $$\\varepsilon(P;x)=\n \\inf_{\\substack{x\\in D\\subset V\\\\D\\text{ an integral curve}}}\n \\frac{P\\cdot D}{\\mathop{\\mathrm{mult}}_xD}.$$ The constants are independent of $V$, $G$, and $P$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-scalar-subadjunction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a projective $\\mathbb Q$-factorial klt variety, let $\\Theta\\ge0$ be a rational divisor, and let $S\\subset Z$ be an integral subvariety of dimension $d>0$. Suppose that $(Z,\\Theta)$ is lc over a neighbourhood of the generic point of $S$, and that $S$ is an lc centre there. For a nef rational Cartier divisor $A$ on $Z$ and a smooth projective resolution $S^*\\to S$, one has $$\\label{eq:scalar-subadjunction}\n K_{S^*}\\cdot(A|_{S^*})^{d-1}\n \\le (K_Z+\\Theta)|_S\\cdot(A|_S)^{d-1}.$$ Here the right-hand side means the intersection of the restricted rational line bundle with the cycle $S$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.13"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-scalar-tracking",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a projective $\\mathbb Q$-factorial klt variety of dimension $n\\ge2$, and let $N$ be a big nef semiample rational Cartier divisor. Suppose there are $n+1$ positive rational numbers, with consecutive spacing $\\delta>0$, all strictly between $(N^n)^{1/n}$ and $\\gamma(N;Z)$. There is a covering family of integral subvarieties $S\\subset Z$ of some dimension $0<d<n$ such that, on a smooth projective resolution $S^*$ of its geometric generic member, $$\\begin{aligned}\n N^d\\cdot S&\\le (2/\\delta)^{n-d}N^n,\n       \\label{eq:scalar-track-degree}\\\\\n K_{S^*}\\cdot(N|_{S^*})^{d-1}\n &\\le \\bigl(K_Z+(2n/\\delta)N\\bigr)|_S\n             \\cdot(N|_S)^{d-1}.\n       \\label{eq:scalar-track-canonical}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.13"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-scalar-iitaka-volume",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $1\\le d\\le3$ and a positive rational number $C'$. Let $(W_i,L_i)$ be a sequence of smooth projective $d$-folds with $\\kappa(W_i)\\ge0$ and big, nef, semiample rational Cartier divisors $L_i$. Suppose that $$\\label{eq:scalar-fibre-hypotheses}\n L_i^d\\longrightarrow0,\n \\qquad K_{W_i}\\cdot L_i^{d-1}\\le C'L_i^d.$$ After passing to a subsequence and resolving the Iitaka fibrations, their smooth geometric generic fibres $U_i$ have a fixed positive dimension, satisfy $\\kappa(U_i)=0$, and obey $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(K_{U_i}+L_i|_{U_i})\\longrightarrow0.$$ Here $L_i$ denotes its pullback to the resolved model; its restriction to $U_i$ is big, nef, and semiample. When $\\kappa(W_i)=0$, the fibre $U_i$ means $W_i$ itself.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.13"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-quadratic-cost",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a normal projective canonical fourfold over $\\mathbb C$ with $K_X\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$, and let $r$ be the least positive integer such that $rK_X\\sim0$. Let $\\pi:Y\\to X$ be its canonical cyclic cover, with group $\\mu_r$. Fix $C_1\\in\\mathbb Q_{>0}$, and choose $b_0\\in\\mathbb Q_{>0}$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:frob-choice-b0}\n 3(2b_0)^4<\\frac14,\n \\qquad b_0(C_1+1)<\\frac34.$$ Suppose that $L$ is an ample rational Cartier divisor on $X$ such that $$1\\le L^4\\le2,\n \\qquad \\gamma(L;X)\\le C_1,$$ where $\\gamma$ is as in Definition <a href=\"#def:orders\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:orders\">18</a>. For each integer $t\\ge2$, set $$Z_t=Y^t/\\mu_{r,\\mathrm{diag}},\n \\qquad\n \\theta_t:Z_t\\longrightarrow X^t,\n \\qquad\n P_t=\\theta_t^*\\left(\\sum_{i=1}^t\\operatorname{pr}_i^*L\\right).$$ Then $P_t$ is ample and, at a very general point $z$ of $Z_t$, $$\\label{eq:frob-cost}\n \\varepsilon(P_t;z)\\le\\frac{5t^2}{b_0}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.13"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-quadratic-cost",

For each integer $t\\ge2$, set $$Z_t=Y^t/\\mu_{r,\\mathrm{diag}},\n \\qquad\n \\theta_t:Z_t\\longrightarrow X^t,\n \\qquad\n P_t=\\theta_t^*\\left(\\sum_{i=1}^t\\operatorname{pr}_i^*L\\right).$$ Then $P_t$ is ample and, at a very general point $z$ of $Z_t$, $$\\label{eq:frob-cost}\n \\varepsilon(P_t;z)\\le\\frac{5t^2}{b_0}.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-frob-truncated-slopes",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a smooth projective integral curve of genus $g$ over an algebraically closed field $k$ of characteristic $p>0$. Let $V$ be a vector bundle of rank $n$ with $\\mu_{\\min}(V)\\ge0$, and put $$G=\\max\\{0,2g-2\\},\\qquad u=n(p-1),\\qquad c_2=n(n-1)G.$$ Let $A_\\bullet(V)$ denote the symmetric algebra of $V$ modulo the ideal locally generated by the $p$-th powers of its linear generators. Thus, in a local frame, $A_\\bullet(V)$ is the graded algebra $\\mathcal O_C[v_1,\\ldots,v_n]/(v_1^p,\\ldots,v_n^p)$. Its graded pieces are locally free, it is zero in degrees above $u$, and $$\\label{eq:frob-truncated-upper-slope}\n \\mu_{\\max}\\bigl(A_j(V)\\bigr)\n \\le (p-1)\\deg(V)+c_2\n \\qquad (0\\le j\\le u).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.13"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-frob-truncated-slopes",
          "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a smooth projective integral curve of genus $g$ over an algebraically closed field $k$ of characteristic $p>0$. Let $V$ be a vector bundle of rank $n$ with $\\mu_{\\min}(V)\\ge0$, and put $$G=\\max\\{0,2g-2\\},\\qquad u=n(p-1),\\qquad c_2=n(n-1)G.$$ Let $A_\\bullet(V)$ denote the symmetric algebra of $V$ modulo the ideal locally generated by the $p$-th powers of its linear generators. Thus, in a local frame, $A_\\bullet(V)$ is the graded algebra $\\mathcal O_C[v_1,\\ldots,v_n]/(v_1^p,\\ldots,v_n^p)$. Its graded pieces are locally free, it is zero in degrees above $u$, and $$\\label{eq:frob-truncated-upper-slope}\n \\mu_{\\max}\\bigl(A_j(V)\\bigr)\n \\le (p-1)\\deg(V)+c_2\n \\qquad (0\\le j\\le u).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-034.13"
        }

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **canonical index** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Its canonical index is the least positive integer $r$ for which $rK_X$ is an integral principal divisor.
- **Iitaka field K(D)** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The Iitaka field $K(D)\subset\mathbb C(X)$ is generated by ratios of nonzero sections in the same $V_m(D)$, over all $m$.
- **complete system giving the Iitaka fibration** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A complete system gives the Iitaka fibration when its ratios generate this entire field.
- **quasi-étale** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Quasi-étale means finite surjective and étale outside codimension at least two.
- **contraction** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A contraction is a surjective projective morphism $f:X\to Z$ of normal varieties with $f_*\mathcal O_X=\mathcal O_Z$.
- **uniform index** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A uniform index means a common positive integer giving an integral linearly trivial multiple, not merely a numerical relation.
- **canonical order** ([source](#sec:index-reductions)): The order of $K_X\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$ is the least positive integer $r$ such that $rK_X\sim0$, with linear equivalence understood for integral Weil divisors.
- **marked covering family** ([source](#pm-h-14)): A *marked covering family* on $V$ consists of an integral parameter space $S$, a dominant marked-point map $u:S\to V$, and a family of subvarieties $C_b\subset V$ containing $u(b)$.
- **generic movement** ([source](#pm-h-14)): Starting from a generic point $a$ of $V$, a *generic movement* chooses one of the marked families, a generic point $b$ of a geometric component of $u^{-1}(a)$ over the field of the existing path, and then a generic endpoint on $C_b$ over that field together with $b$.
- **chain leaves** ([source](#pm-h-5)): The closures of the generically reachable sets are fibres of a rational quotient, called *chain leaves*.
- **Seshadri constant** ([source](#pm-h-4)): The infimum over such curves is the Seshadri constant $\varepsilon(P;x)$.
- **canonical cyclic cover** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A rational function trivializing $rK_V$ defines its canonical cyclic cover $\pi:Y\to V$, a connected degree-$r$ quasi-étale cover with deck group $\mu_r$ and trivial canonical class upstairs.
- **product polarization P_t** ([source](#pm-h-4)): The polarization $P_t$ is the pullback by $\theta_t$ of the sum of the $t$ copies of $L$.
- **generalized pair** ([source](#sec:relative-inputs)): A generalized pair $(Z,B_Z+M_Z)$ includes a rational b-divisor $\mathbf M$ that is the pullback of a nef rational Cartier divisor on a higher projective model.
- **normalized section order and volume root** ([source](#stmt-def-orders)): Let $V$ be an integral projective variety of positive dimension, and let $P$ be a big, nef, semiample rational Cartier divisor on $V$. For a smooth projective birational resolution $\pi:\widetilde V\to V$, set $$\gamma(P;V)= \sup_{\substack{k>0\text{ sufficiently divisible}\\ 0\ne s\in H^0(\widetilde V,k\pi^*P)}} \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_x(s)}{k}, \qquad \rho(P;V)=(P^{\dim V})^{1/\dim V},$$ where $x$ is very general in the smooth isomorphism locus, identified with its lift to $\widetilde V$. If a specified smooth point $x$ is used instead, write $\gamma(P;V,x)$, with the resolution chosen to be an isomorphism near $x$. For a subvariety $A$, the notation $\gamma(P;A)$ means the same invariant for the restriction of $P$, pulled back to a resolution of $A$.
- **truncated symmetric algebra** ([source](#stmt-lem-frob-truncated-slopes)): Let $A_\bullet(V)$ denote the symmetric algebra of $V$ modulo the ideal locally generated by the $p$-th powers of its linear generators.
- **effective comparison preceq** ([source](#sec:scalar)): For rational Cartier classes $A,B$, we write $A\preceq B$ when $B-A$ is rationally linearly equivalent to an effective rational divisor.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-index-n4 | uniform canonical index $N_4$ | = | $N_4$ | independent of X |
| q-degree-m-phi | uniform Iitaka degree | = | $m(\Phi)$ | depends only on the finite coefficient set |
| q-effective-base-degree | uniform degree over a big base | = | $m(d,\Phi)$ | depends on d and Phi only |
| q-rc-torsion-ell | uniform torsion multiple on rationally connected bases | = | $\ell(d,I,p)$ | depends on d, I and p |
| q-scalar-order-upper | normalized section order $\gamma(P;V)$ | ≤ | $C(P^4)^{1/4}$ | constants independent of V, G and P |
| q-scalar-seshadri-lower | curve ratio $\varepsilon(P;x)$ | ≥ | $c(P^4)^{1/4}$ | constants independent of V, G and P |
| q-leaf-degree | leaf degree | ≤ | $(eB)^e$ | marked curve ratios at most B |
| q-quadratic-cost | curve ratio on the diagonal quotient | ≤ | $\frac{5t^2}{b_0}$ | independent of the cover order |
| q-frob-slope | maximal slope of graded pieces of the truncated symmetric algebra | ≤ | $(p-1)\deg(V)+c_2$ | uniform in the graded degree |
| q-external-order | order of a section of an external product at a product point | ≤ | $\sum_{i=1}^t a_i$ | a_i are maximal slot orders |
| q-index-g0 | index of $G_0$ in $G$ | ≤ | $4!$ | fourfold case |
| q-totient-bound | Euler function of the order of the canonical character | ≤ | $70$ | fourfolds |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-pole-test** [claim](#stmt-prop-semiample-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-semiample-model): floor-pole equivalence for rational functions and exceptional comparison -> equality of rounded section spaces under crepant and effective exceptional comparisons
- **step-semiample-model** [claim](#stmt-prop-semiample-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-semiample-model): dlt modification then adjoint-negative program -> semiample dlt model with the same section spaces
- **step-abundance** [claim](#stmt-thm-fourfold) / [proof](#stmt-thm-fourfold): cited companion theorem -> semiampleness after nonvanishing
- **step-multiples** [claim](#stmt-lem-multiples-full-field) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-multiples-full-field): multiplication by a fixed section -> generation preserved in all multiples
- **step-relative-inputs** [claim](#stmt-thm-relative-denominators) / [proof](#stmt-thm-relative-denominators): cited companion results -> uniform denominators
- **step-effective-base** [claim](#stmt-prop-effective-base) / [proof](#stmt-prop-effective-base): cited companion result -> uniform degree generating base field
- **step-rc-torsion** [claim](#stmt-prop-rc-torsion) / [proof](#stmt-prop-rc-torsion): cited companion result -> uniform torsion
- **step-product-case** [claim](#stmt-prop-index-product-case) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-index-product-case): block characters and cohomology rank bound -> uniform multiple for product and abelian cases
- **step-single-factor** [claim](#stmt-lem-index-single-factor-images) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-index-single-factor-images): generic semipositivity and form algebra of the factor -> no non-uniruled images
- **step-exceptional-psef** [claim](#stmt-lem-index-exceptional-psef) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-index-exceptional-psef): Hodge index and negativity lemma -> effectivity
- **step-noncanonical** [claim](#stmt-cor-index-noncanonical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-index-noncanonical): uniruledness criterion then rationally connected torsion -> bounded order
- **step-terminalization** [claim](#stmt-lem-index-terminalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-index-terminalization): extraction of finitely many valuations -> terminal crepant model with same order
- **step-moving-divisor** [claim](#stmt-lem-index-moving-divisor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-index-moving-divisor): semiample contraction then relative denominators and torsion -> bounded order
- **step-general-type-fibres** [claim](#stmt-lem-index-general-type-fibres) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-index-general-type-fibres): descent of invariant fields and bigness on the fibre -> general type fibre
- **step-generic-chain** [claim](#stmt-lem-generic-chain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-generic-chain): stabilization of endpoint loci -> rational quotient with leaves
- **step-chain-functoriality** [claim](#stmt-cor-chain-functoriality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-chain-functoriality): conjugation invariance of names -> projection and quotient compatibility
- **step-chain-order** [claim](#stmt-lem-chain-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chain-order): parameter differentiation of jet kernels -> leaf order and degree bounds
- **step-subadjunction** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-subadjunction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-subadjunction): dlt blow-up and canonical bundle formula -> numerical subadjunction
- **step-tracking** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-tracking) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-tracking): moving base component and lc threshold -> covering family with bounds
- **step-covering** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-covering) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-covering): generically finite evaluation of cut incidence -> Kodaira dimension of members
- **step-iitaka-volume** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-iitaka-volume) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-iitaka-volume): weak positivity on Iitaka fibrations -> small adjoint volume on fibres
- **step-scalar-upper** [claim](#stmt-thm-scalar-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-iitaka-volume-2): dimension descent contradicting general type leaf -> upper order bound
- **step-scalar-lower** [claim](#stmt-thm-scalar-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-iitaka-volume-3): thickening of a very general fibre -> lower curve ratio bound
- **step-frob-slopes** [claim](#stmt-lem-frob-truncated-slopes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-frob-truncated-slopes): canonical connection and perfect pairing duality -> slope bound
- **step-frob-flag** [claim](#stmt-lem-frob-flag-sections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-frob-flag-sections): divisor exact sequences along a flag -> section bound
- **step-frob-external** [claim](#stmt-lem-frob-external-orders) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-frob-external-orders): Künneth and adapted order filtrations -> order bound for products
- **step-quadratic-cost** [claim](#stmt-prop-quadratic-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-frob-external-orders-2): reduction to positive characteristic and diagonal rank deficit against determinant order -> quadratic curve cost
- **step-many-factor** [claim](#stmt-prop-many-factor-contradiction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-many-factor-contradiction): stabilization of leaf degrees and flows of transported vector fields -> uncountable family of birational maps
- **step-countability** [claim](#stmt-lem-birational-countability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-birational-countability): countable class group and Matsumura obstruction -> countable birational group
- **step-index-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-fourfold-index-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-birational-countability-2): case split and contradiction on unbounded orders -> uniform integer N_4
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-birational-countability-3): nonvanishing, semiample model, then base field or index cases -> uniform degree generating the Iitaka field

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-explicit-b0-choice**: For every positive rational C_1, the explicit choice b_0=1/(4(C_1+1)) satisfies both strict conditions 3(2b_0)^4<1/4 and b_0(C_1+1)<3/4 required in the quadratic cost proposition.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-wrong-dimension**: Invoke the uniform degree theorem for a variety whose dimension is not four. -> The uniform degree is proved only for fourfolds; for other dimensions the integer may not exist or may depend on more data.
- **anti-unnormalised-volume**: Use the explicit b_0 recipe with a polarization whose top self-intersection is not normalized. -> The cost bound is proved only after rescaling the polarization into a fixed volume window, so the recipe's constants say nothing outside it.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. Only one finite explicit computation appears in the paper: the closed-form choice of the rational b_0 from C_1 whose two strict inequalities are checked in exact arithmetic. The main existence theorems are not finite procedures and are not covered.

```python
# INPUTS: one line on stdin holding a positive rational C1 written as p/q
# PRECONDITIONS: C1 is a positive rational number
# OUTPUTS: one line holding the rational b0 = 1/(4(C1+1)) in lowest terms
# TIME: constant number of exact rational operations
# SPACE: constant
# ARITHMETIC: exact Fraction arithmetic, no floating point
# FAILURE: malformed or nonpositive input raises an exception and exits nonzero
import sys
from fractions import Fraction
c1 = Fraction(sys.stdin.read().strip())
assert c1 > 0
b0 = Fraction(1) / (4 * (c1 + 1))
print(b0)
```

| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The index theorem asserts that some integer N_4 exists for all klt fourfolds with rationally trivial canonical divisor. It supplies no procedure, input format or output that a program could execute, and no value of N_4 is given. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The chain and leaf constructions live on abstract integral projective varieties in characteristic zero and rely on geometric generic points. There is no discrete or numerical state space that a simulation could step through. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A computer-algebra tool could in principle want the degree m(Phi) to truncate section computations. The theorem only states that such a positive integer exists for a finite coefficient set, with no explicit value or algorithm to find it. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The semiample-model comparison uses a crepant dlt modification followed by adjoint-negative birational steps, with termination cited from the literature. No explicit step-by-step procedure with bounded cost is stated, so there is nothing to implement or analyse for complexity. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The objects are reflexive sheaves, jet kernels and flags of complete-intersection curves. These are not finite combinatorial structures with insert or query operations, so no data structure is defined or implied. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The quadratic jet-cost estimate bounds a Seshadri-type invariant of a diagonal product of covers. It is a geometric inequality with no arithmetic kernel, dataflow or memory-access pattern that hardware could accelerate. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Reduction to positive characteristic is used only as a proof device for rank and slope estimates. The paper defines no primitive, hardness assumption or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The abundance statement is a qualitative theorem about semiample divisors on log canonical pairs over the complex numbers. It involves no data, loss, training procedure or learnable parameters. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The scalar bounds assert that universal constants C and c exist. There is no objective function, feasibility set or solution method, and the constants are not computed or optimised. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The countability statement concerns the group of birational self-maps of a terminal variety. It has no nodes, messages, failure model or consensus structure, so no distributed protocol follows from it. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result belongs to pure higher-dimensional algebraic geometry and contributes a theorem for further research in that field. It is a non-constructive existence of uniform degrees and gives no engineering artefact. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-finite-rational-coefficients** (hypothesis): The uniform degree is stated only for a finite set of rational boundary coefficients in the unit interval. The paper explicitly disclaims any assertion for arbitrary real coefficient sets, and its theorem does not extend to infinite descending-chain-condition coefficient sets. Blocked use: ['Quoting the uniform degree for pairs whose boundary coefficients are real, irrational or drawn from an infinite set.', 'Treating the finite-coefficient theorem as the full effective log Iitaka conjecture for descending-chain-condition coefficient sets.']
- **limit-dimension-four-lc-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The uniform Iitaka degree is proved for normal integral projective complex fourfolds carrying a log canonical pair with effective rational boundary and a rational Cartier, pseudo-effective adjoint. Every one of these conditions is used, and the result gives no information outside this setting. Blocked use: ['Invoking the degree for varieties of any dimension other than four.', 'Applying it to non-normal, non-projective or non-complex varieties, or to pairs that are not log canonical.', 'Applying it when the adjoint is not rational Cartier or not pseudo-effective.']
- **limit-nonvanishing-degree-not-uniform** (formal_scope): Passing from pseudo-effectivity to a nonzero section supplies a section in some positive degree only. That degree is not uniform and is not controlled by the coefficient set; uniformity comes only from the index and fibration arguments. Blocked use: ['Using the nonvanishing step to bound the degree at which sections first appear.', 'Reading the qualitative nonvanishing input as an effective statement.']
- **limit-existence-only-index-and-degree** (nonconstructive): The canonical index bound and hence the uniform Iitaka degree are obtained by contradiction from a hypothetical sequence of unbounded orders. The argument proves that a bound exists and produces no explicit value or procedure for computing it. Blocked use: ['Hard-coding a numerical value for the index bound or for the uniform degree.', 'Using the theorems as an algorithm that outputs the trivializing multiple for a given fourfold.']
- **limit-scalar-bounds-fibration-condition** (hypothesis): The scalar upper and lower bounds hold only for canonical fourfolds with rationally trivial canonical divisor under the stated finite group action and only when every equivariant rational fibration with positive-dimensional base and fibre has a general-type geometric generic fibre. In the index argument this condition is verified separately and is not automatic. Blocked use: ['Applying the section-order and curve-ratio bounds to a fourfold where an equivariant fibration has a generic fibre not of general type.', 'Treating the constants in the scalar bounds as available without checking the fibration condition.']
- **limit-arithmetic-model-not-uniform** (approximation): The positive-characteristic reduction is a proof device applied to a fixed finite list of data. The arithmetic model is fixed for each variety, cover and product size before the characteristic grows, and no single arithmetic model is claimed for all cover orders. Blocked use: ['Treating the reductions as constructions over finite fields usable in a protocol or a computation.', 'Claiming a characteristic threshold that is uniform in the order of the cyclic cover.']
- **limit-companion-subadditivity-input** (formal_scope): The full pseudo-effective statement depends on the companion good-model result for nonvanishing and inherits its logarithmic Iitaka subadditivity input. The semiample-model and index arguments alone do not need that input, so the final theorem is conditional on the companion in this respect. Blocked use: ['Presenting the full pseudo-effective theorem as independent of the companion log abundance results.', 'Quoting the semiample-model comparison alone as proving nonvanishing from pseudo-effectivity.']
- **limit-image-dimension-not-iitaka-fibration** (edge_case): Obtaining the correct image dimension from a linear system does not show that the system gives the Iitaka fibration, since a finite extension of the function field may remain. The theorem requires the section ratios to generate the whole Iitaka field. Blocked use: ['Declaring a system to give the Iitaka fibration because its image has dimension equal to the Kodaira dimension.', 'Truncating a complete rounded system on the basis of image dimension alone.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the finite-rational-coefficient case of the effective log Iitaka conjecture in dimension four. For normal projective log canonical complex fourfolds with boundary coefficients in a fixed finite rational set and pseudo-effective rational Cartier adjoint, one uniform degree makes the complete rounded reflexive system nonempty and its section ratios generate the full Iitaka field. We also prove a uniform canonical index bound for projective klt complex fourfolds with rationally trivial canonical divisor.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $X$ be a normal projective klt variety with $K_X\sim_\mathbb Q0$. Its canonical index is the least positive integer $r$ for which $rK_X$ is an integral principal divisor. The index problem asks for a bound depending only on the dimension. More generally, for a log Calabi–Yau pair one allows dependence on the boundary coefficients. In dimension three, Jiang obtains a common canonical multiple for all klt Calabi–Yau threefolds, using boundedness modulo flops to handle the noncanonical case <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Jiang2021](#ref-Jiang2021), Theorem 1.6 and Corollary 1.7]. Xu’s inductive results give the lc threefold index theorem and the klt fourfold theorem with nonzero boundary <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Xu2019](#ref-Xu2019), Theorems 1.13–1.14]. Jiang and Haidong Liu bound surface log pluricanonical representations and, through Fujino’s gluing framework, obtain the index theorem for slc pairs through dimension three and connected non-klt lc fourfold pairs <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[JiangLiu2021](#ref-JiangLiu2021), Theorem 1.4 and Corollaries 1.6–1.7]. Birkar also proves an index bound in every fixed dimension for rationally connected klt log Calabi–Yau pairs with coefficients in a fixed rational set satisfying the descending chain condition (DCC) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Birkar2025](#ref-Birkar2025), Corollary 1.8]. The distinction between zero and nonzero boundary, and between rationally connected and non-uniruled varieties, is therefore central to the remaining fourfold argument.

<a id="stmt-thm-fourfold-index-zero"></a>
<div id="thm:fourfold-index-zero" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Uniform canonical index). *There is a positive integer $N_4$ such that $N_4K_X\sim0$ for every normal projective klt complex fourfold $X$ with $K_X\sim_\mathbb Q0$.*

</div>

The conclusion concerns integral Weil divisors: it gives a trivialization in one degree for all the fourfolds in the statement. Combined with the preceding non-klt and nonzero-boundary results, it gives the lc fourfold index theorem for any fixed finite rational coefficient set. In applying Xu’s theorem, a finite set is included in the hyperstandard set generated by its complementary coefficients. No assertion for arbitrary real coefficient sets is intended.

Our effective application concerns the map determined by a log canonical divisor. For a rational Cartier divisor $D$ on a normal integral projective variety, fix a divisor representative and put $$V_m(D)=H^0\bigl(X,\mathcal O_X(\lfloor mD\rfloor)\bigr),\qquad m\geq1.$$ The sheaf here is the rank-one reflexive divisorial sheaf, so this definition does not require $mD$ to be Cartier. The Iitaka field $K(D)\subset\mathbb C(X)$ is generated by ratios of nonzero sections in the same $V_m(D)$, over all $m$. A complete system gives the Iitaka fibration when its ratios generate this entire field. Merely obtaining the correct image dimension would not rule out a finite extension.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Uniform effective log Iitaka fibrations). *Let $\Phi\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$ be finite. There is a positive integer $m=m(\Phi)$ such that the following holds. Suppose $X$ is a normal integral projective complex fourfold, $\Delta\geq0$ is a rational Weil divisor whose nonzero coefficients lie in $\Phi$, $(X,\Delta)$ is lc, and $D=K_X+\Delta$ is rational Cartier and pseudo-effective. Then the complete system $|\lfloor mD\rfloor|$ is nonempty and its section ratios generate $K(D)$ inside $\mathbb C(X)$. The same integer works for every choice of canonical divisor on $X$.*

</div>

In Kodaira dimension four the system is birational; in Kodaira dimension zero it is nonempty with image a point. Nonvanishing follows from pseudo-effectivity by the good-model theorem and effective exceptional comparison in OpenAI, *Log abundance in characteristic zero <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[OpenAIAbundance](#ref-OpenAIAbundance), Theorems 1.1 and 11.1, Lemma 2.2]* . This qualitative step supplies a section in some positive degree, not a uniform degree; the uniform $m(\Phi)$ comes from the index and fibration arguments below.

Earlier effective results separate the big case from lower-dimensional Iitaka bases. Hacon–McKernan–Xu give a uniform birational degree for big lc adjoints with coefficients in any fixed DCC set <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[HMX2014](#ref-HMX2014), Theorem 1.3]. Viehweg–Zhang treat smooth varieties of Kodaira dimension two. Their degree depends on invariants of the general fibre; for fourfolds the fibre is a surface of Kodaira dimension zero, and boundedness of those invariants makes the degree universal <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[ViehwegZhang2009](#ref-ViehwegZhang2009), Theorem 0.2 and the discussion preceding Corollary 0.4]. Todorov–Xu treat klt pairs of log Kodaira codimension two in dimensions two, three and four, with coefficients in a rational DCC set <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[TodorovXu2009](#ref-TodorovXu2009), Theorem 1.2]. For fourfolds their hypothesis is $\kappa(K_X+\Delta)=2$. Chen, Han and Jihao Liu formulate the effective log Iitaka conjecture for lc pairs of nonnegative Kodaira dimension with coefficients in an arbitrary DCC subset of $[0,1]$, and prove it through dimension three <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[ChenHanLiu2023](#ref-ChenHanLiu2023), Conjecture 1.2 and Corollary 1.5].

The canonical bundle formula relates this question to effective birationality on the base. Birkar–Zhang prove effective Iitaka fibrations for smooth projective varieties of nonnegative Kodaira dimension, with constants depending on the dimension, the least nonvanishing pluricanonical degree on the general fibre, and the middle Betti number of a smooth model of its canonical cover. In fixed dimension and for a fixed DCC coefficient set, their polarized effective birationality theorem gives a uniform degree for a big base adjoint once the nef part has bounded denominator <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BirkarZhang2016](#ref-BirkarZhang2016), Theorems 1.2–1.3]. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a> gives the fourfold conclusion for finite rational coefficient sets, across all nonnegative Kodaira dimensions and for lc singularities. Its coefficient scope is narrower than that of the DCC conjecture and the earlier big and klt results. The uniform degree uses the canonical-index theorem and the relative denominator theorem below. The passage from pseudo-effectivity to nonvanishing is the separate qualitative input described above.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The model comparison and the index reductions

The uniform argument needs a model on which the adjoint defines a morphism. The comparison in Proposition <a href="#prop:semiample-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:semiample-model">4</a> preserves *every* space $V_m(D)$ under a crepant dlt modification and an adjoint-negative program. Its semiample output uses the abundance-after-nonvanishing theorem of OpenAI, *Lifting sections from the reduced support of an adjoint <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[OpenAILifting](#ref-OpenAILifting), Theorem]* . That theorem applies to projective lc rational pairs over $\mathbb C$ of dimension at most four: a nef rational Cartier adjoint of nonnegative Kodaira dimension is semiample. In Kodaira dimension zero it is rationally linearly trivial. We state the model comparison before the index reduction because it is needed there as well as in the final effective application.

The singular Beauville–Bogomolov decomposition gives a finite quasi-étale cover by a product of an abelian variety and nonflat Calabi–Yau or symplectic factors. Druel proves the needed dimension-at-most-five result <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Druel2018](#ref-Druel2018), Theorem 1.2]; Höring–Peternell prove the arbitrary-dimensional version <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[HoringPeternell2019](#ref-HoringPeternell2019), Theorem 1.5], using Druel’s integrability results and the holonomy decomposition of Greb–Guenancia–Kebekus <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[GGK2019](#ref-GGK2019), Theorem B and Proposition D]. Here quasi-étale means étale outside a subset of codimension at least two. Integral cohomology bounds the abelian characters, and lower-dimensional pluricanonical characters handle products. The remaining single-factor case has rationally connected proper rational images. Its noncanonical quotients are handled by a relative torsion bound; canonical quotients admit a crepant terminal model $V$ with the same canonical index, irregularity zero and countable birational self-map group.

An effective divisor $D$ of Iitaka dimension one, two or three on $V$ would give another bounded-index case. A small rational boundary $\delta D$ produces a semiample contraction, but the relative index theorem is applied only after proving that the underlying canonical divisors remain crepant. The resulting fibration has boundary zero, so the relative denominator and rationally connected torsion theorems <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[OpenAIRelative](#ref-OpenAIRelative), Theorem and Proposition] have constants independent of $D$ and $\delta$. Consequently, in the unbounded-index case every effective divisor of positive Iitaka dimension is big. This implies the precise fibration property used in the scalar estimate: every intermediate equivariant rational fibration of the canonical cover has a geometric generic fibre of general type on a smooth projective resolution. It is a conclusion of the reduction.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Curves on the canonical cover and its products

Let $r$ be the canonical index of the remaining terminal fourfold $V$. A rational function trivializing $rK_V$ defines its canonical cyclic cover $\pi:Y\to V$, a connected degree-$r$ quasi-étale cover with deck group $\mu_r$ and trivial canonical class upstairs. The minimality of $r$ is what makes this the primitive cover. Choose an ample rational divisor $L$ on $V$, scaled so that its volume is bounded above and below by absolute positive constants. Then $(\pi^*L)^4=rL^4$.

For an ample rational divisor $P$, two local measurements will be used. The number $\gamma(P;V)$ is the supremum of the order of vanishing of a section in degree $k$, divided by $k$, at a very general smooth point; degrees are sufficiently divisible to make the divisor Cartier. Its precise definition is given in Definition <a href="#def:orders" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:orders">18</a>. For an integral curve $C$ through a smooth marked point $x$, the ratio $$\frac{P\cdot C}{\operatorname{mult}_x C}$$ compares its degree with its multiplicity at that point. The infimum over such curves is the Seshadri constant $\varepsilon(P;x)$. The scalar estimates bound $\gamma$ above and $\varepsilon$ below by absolute multiples of the fourth root of the volume, under the fibration hypothesis just obtained. Applied upstairs, the lower bound makes every such curve ratio grow at least as $r^{1/4}$.

Now take $t$ copies of $Y$ and divide by the simultaneous deck action: $$Z_t=Y^t/\mu_{r,\mathrm{diag}},\qquad
 \theta_t:Z_t\longrightarrow V^t.$$ The polarization $P_t$ is the pullback by $\theta_t$ of the sum of the $t$ copies of $L$. On $Z_t$, the product estimate gives curves through very general points whose degree divided by marked multiplicity is bounded by a constant times $t^2$, independently of $r$. This estimate concerns the quotient by the diagonal group; it does not assert the same bound on $Y^t$. The maps are displayed in Figure <a href="#fig:diagonal-cover" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:diagonal-cover">1</a>.

<figure id="fig:diagonal-cover">

<figcaption>The product polarization is pulled back through $θ_{t}$. The geometric generic fibre of $q_{1}$ is $Y^{t − 1}$: choosing one lift of the marked point in $V$ removes the simultaneous deck action. This identification allows the growing scalar curve bound upstairs to be tested inside a product whose curve cost is bounded independently of $r$.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-diagonal-cover"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:diagonal-cover`)

Visual witness description of PDF page 4 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

```text
A commutative diagram containing six objects in two rows and three columns: top row displays $Y^t$, $Z_t$, and $V^t$; bottom row displays $Y$, $V$, and $V$. Horizontal arrows: top row has an arrow from $Y^t$ to $Z_t$ labeled $/\mu_{r,\mathrm{diag}}$ and an arrow from $Z_t$ to $V^t$ labeled $\theta_t$; bottom row has an arrow from $Y$ to $V$ labeled $\pi$, followed by an equality sign ($=$) connecting the middle $V$ and right $V$. Vertical downward arrows: from $Y^t$ to $Y$ labeled $\mathrm{pr}_1$, from $Z_t$ to $V$ labeled $q_1$, and from $V^t$ to $V$ labeled $\mathrm{pr}_1$.
```


The product estimate is proved by reducing a fixed set of data to positive characteristic and comparing ordinary jets with Frobenius jets. The local comparison is that of Mustaţă–Schwede <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[MustataSchwede2014](#ref-MustataSchwede2014), Equation (2.8)]. The canonical filtration described by Sun <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Sun2008](#ref-Sun2008), Theorem 3.7 and Lemma 4.2], in the ideal-theoretic description of Kitadai–Sumihiro <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[KitadaiSumihiro2008](#ref-KitadaiSumihiro2008), Definition 3.1 and Remark 3.2], supplies the truncated symmetric powers and their determinant factors. We prove the slope estimates and the diagonal rank comparison needed for these products. For each variety, cover and product size, the arithmetic model is fixed before the characteristic tends to infinity. The later contradiction fixes a finite list of product sizes before allowing $r$ to grow. These are separate limits; neither requires a uniform arithmetic model for all $r$.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### From chains of curves to birational maps

The curve estimates concern different spaces. We compare them by forming chains from finitely many covering families of marked curves on $Z_t$, chosen compatibly with coordinate projections and the group actions. Each movement follows a chosen curve from its marked point to a generic point. The closures of the generically reachable sets are fibres of a rational quotient, called *chain leaves*. The construction uses Campana’s stabilization strategy <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Campana2004](#ref-Campana2004), Theorem 1.1 and its proof sketch]; parameter differentiation in the manner of Ein–Küchle–Lazarsfeld <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[EinKuchleLazarsfeld1995](#ref-EinKuchleLazarsfeld1995), Proposition 2.3] retains a degree bound. In our setting a generic point of an $e$-dimensional leaf can be reached in at most $e$ movements. If the marked curve ratios are at most $B$, the leaf degree is at most $(eB)^e$. We prove the projection and finite-quotient compatibilities needed to use these bounds together.

Write $H_t\subset Z_t$ for a generic leaf and $W_t=\theta_t(H_t)\subset V^t$. A positive-dimensional fibre of $H_t\to V$ would lie, after choosing one lift of the marked base point, in $Y^{t-1}$. The scalar lower bound there grows with $r$, contradicting the bounded leaf degree. Thus, for sufficiently large $r$, the maps to each coordinate are generically finite onto their images and the leaf dimensions lie between one and four. Among a sufficiently long finite list of product sizes, stabilization of dimensions and a comparison of forgetting-map degrees force one map $W_{t+1}\to W_t$ to have degree one.

This birational forgetting map produces more than a dimension contradiction. The leaf tangent spaces identify positive-dimensional subspaces $A_x\subset T_xV$ at different points. We show that the resulting linear transports depend only on their two endpoints and compose. A vector transported from one fixed point then defines a rational vector field. Degree-one forgetting supplies rational evaluation of its local flow, and inverse flow gives birational self-maps. The resulting uncountable family contradicts the countability of $\mathop{\mathrm{Bir}}(V)$, completing the index argument.

Finally, the model comparison turns the effective theorem into a question on the base of a semiample contraction. For every positive-dimensional base the relative big-base theorem <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[OpenAIRelative](#ref-OpenAIRelative), Proposition] gives the whole base field in a uniform complete floor system, including when the contraction is birational. In Kodaira dimension zero, the three index cases give a nonzero section in one uniform degree. The comparison of all integer degrees brings these systems back to the original pair. The relative results used in this argument are proved independently of Theorem <a href="#thm:fourfold-index-zero" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:fourfold-index-zero">1</a>. The abundance-after-nonvanishing input from <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[OpenAILifting](#ref-OpenAILifting)] and the uniform and index arguments above do not require logarithmic or orbifold subadditivity. The full pseudo-effective statement of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a>, however, first uses <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[OpenAIAbundance](#ref-OpenAIAbundance)] to obtain nonvanishing and inherits that companion’s logarithmic Iitaka subadditivity input.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Organization and conventions

Section <a href="#sec:reductions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reductions">2</a> constructs the semiample model and compares all rounded systems. Section <a href="#sec:relative-inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:relative-inputs">3</a> records the exact relative inputs. Section <a href="#sec:index-reductions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:index-reductions">4</a> removes product and rationally connected cases and proves the fibration condition needed by the scalar estimate. Sections <a href="#sec:chains" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:chains">5</a>–<a href="#sec:transports" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transports">8</a> prove the chain, scalar, product and transport statements in that order. Section <a href="#sec:index-zero" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:index-zero">9</a> applies them to the remaining canonical covers. Section <a href="#sec:main-proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:main-proof">10</a> derives the effective theorem.

Varieties are over $\mathbb C$ unless a geometric generic fibre or an arithmetic reduction is specified. Canonical divisors in a birational or finite comparison are chosen compatibly by rational top forms. We distinguish linear equivalence $\sim$, rational linear equivalence $\sim_{\mathbb Q}$, and numerical equivalence $\equiv$. A pair has an effective rational boundary and a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier actual adjoint. Subpairs may have negative boundary coefficients. Log discrepancies are ordinary discrepancies plus one; canonical and terminal singularities for zero boundary are defined by nonnegative and positive ordinary discrepancies on exceptional divisors. We use the usual dlt and slc conventions, including conductor gluing in slc adjunction; see <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Kollar2013](#ref-Kollar2013)].

A contraction is a surjective projective morphism $f:X\to Z$ of normal varieties with $f_*\mathcal O_X=\mathcal O_Z$. A rational fibration is understood through a relatively algebraically closed base field inside the total function field. A log Calabi–Yau adjoint is $\mathbb Q$-linearly trivial. A uniform index means a common positive integer giving an integral linearly trivial multiple, not merely a numerical relation. Quasi-étale means finite surjective and étale outside codimension at least two. Rational connectedness on singular spaces refers to a smooth projective model when used in a birational argument.

Divisorial sheaves test rational sections at prime divisors. In particular, $v\ne0$ belongs to $H^0(X,\mathcal O_X(\lfloor mD\rfloor))$ precisely when $\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(v)+mD\geq0$. Numerical positivity of a rational divisor always concerns its rational Cartier class. Section-order and volume limits may be taken in sufficiently divisible degrees: powering a section preserves its normalized order. DCC and ACC stand for the descending and ascending chain conditions.

<a id="sec:reductions"></a>
### Semiample models and rounded section spaces

We pass from the original pair to a semiample model while preserving every rounded section space. The following comparison keeps every integer degree, even when the corresponding adjoint multiple is not Cartier.

We use the following precise theorem of the companion *Lifting sections from the reduced support of an adjoint <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[OpenAILifting](#ref-OpenAILifting), Theorem]* .

<a id="stmt-thm-fourfold"></a>
<div id="thm:fourfold" class="theorem">

**Theorem 3** (Abundance after nonvanishing). *Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a projective lc rational pair over $\mathbb C$ of dimension at most four, with effective boundary and nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier adjoint $D=K_X+\Delta$. If $\kappa(X,D)\ge0$, then $D$ is semiample. If $\kappa(X,D)=0$, then $D\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$.*

</div>

This is the companion’s independent after-nonvanishing theorem. Its proof uses the supported-boundary theorem and lower-dimensional abundance; it does not use the separate fourfold nonvanishing companion. The boundary coefficients may be arbitrary rational numbers in $[0,1]$. The conclusion is qualitative: it supplies neither a uniform index nor a uniform section degree. If a generated Cartier multiple has Iitaka dimension zero, its morphism has image a point and a generating section is nowhere zero, which explains the actual rational linear triviality.

For the original pair we first run an adjoint-negative program and compare all section spaces. For the moving-divisor application in Lemma <a href="#lem:index-moving-divisor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:index-moving-divisor">14</a>, the same procedure applies to a small rational perturbation $(V,\delta D)$. There it supplies only a contraction. Uniform statements enter after the boundary is restored to zero and canonical crepancy is proved separately.

<a id="stmt-prop-semiample-model"></a>
<div id="prop:semiample-model" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:all-rounded-sections"></a>
**Proposition 4**. *Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a projective lc rational pair of dimension four over $\mathbb C$, with $D=K_X+\Delta$ $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and $\kappa(X,D)\geq0$. There is a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt pair $(X',\Delta')$ with semiample adjoint $D'=K_{X'}+\Delta'$ such that, under the identification of rational function fields and compatible canonical divisors, $$\label{eq:all-rounded-sections}
 H^0\bigl(X,\mathcal O_X(\lfloor mD\rfloor)\bigr)
 =H^0\bigl(X',\mathcal O_{X'}(\lfloor mD'\rfloor)\bigr)
 \qquad(m\geq1).$$ The coefficients of $\Delta'$ belong to those of $\Delta$ together with $1$. The map is obtained by a crepant dlt modification followed by adjoint-negative birational steps. For a $\mathbb Q$-factorial klt input the latter steps can be taken directly, with klt outputs. The semiample contraction $f:X'\to Z$ has $\dim Z=\kappa(X,D)$ and $$D'\sim_{\mathbb Q}f^*A$$ for an ample rational Cartier divisor $A$ on the normal projective variety $Z$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-semiample-model"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-semiample-model</summary>

<a id="eq:floor-pole-test"></a>
